THIRD-PARTY NOTICES
===================

edytlab (https://github.com/laadtushar/edytlab) is free software under the MIT
License (the LICENSE file in its source repository). It is built from software
written by other people, who released it under licences that ask to be
credited and to have their licence text and copyright notice passed on. This
file is that notice. It is part of every installer and is shown in the app,
under Settings, as "Open source licences".

What is in it, in order:

  1. CREDITS: the code edytlab's Rust files were derived from (OpenAI Whisper,
     Demucs, pyannote.audio, SciPy, torchaudio, Kaldi) with their licences,
     the changes edytlab made, the models the app can download on request
     (and who made them), and the test data in the source repository.
  2. JAVASCRIPT PACKAGES AND FONTS: what the window of the app is built from,
     including the Geist fonts (SIL Open Font License 1.1).
  3. RUST CRATES: the libraries the program is linked against, for Linux,
     Windows and macOS, with each crate's licence text and copyright holder.
  4. SOURCE AVAILABILITY (MPL-2.0): where to get the source code of the
     libraries under the Mozilla Public License 2.0 (the symphonia audio
     decoders, among others).

Notices that ship in their own files next to this one: ONNX Runtime
(licenses/onnxruntime/LICENSE and ThirdPartyNotices.txt) and, in the Windows
installers, the Microsoft Visual C++ runtime (licenses/vc-runtime/NOTICE.txt).

edytlab's own source code is public, at https://github.com/laadtushar/edytlab,
and is the corresponding source of the program.

This file is generated (scripts/third-party-notices/generate.mjs) from the
dependency lockfiles; do not edit it by hand. It is provided for information
and is not legal advice.

================================================================================

CREDITS
=======

A. Code that edytlab's own source is derived from
-------------------------------------------------

These parts of edytlab are translations into Rust of functions from the
projects below (the algorithm, the constants and, where a file's own
documentation says so, the order of operations are theirs). The Rust files
are not the upstream files: they were written for edytlab, without PyTorch,
numba or SciPy, and each says at its top what it was derived from and what it
does differently. The licence text of every project follows in section F.

  OpenAI Whisper - https://github.com/openai/whisper
    release 20250625, commit 31243bad24cc746f07d4c8bfdd2d974872cb1803
    MIT License, Copyright (c) 2022 OpenAI
    derived: log-mel spectrogram (whisper/audio.py), the decoding loop
    (whisper/decoding.py), segments and word timestamps (whisper/transcribe.py,
    whisper/timing.py: find_alignment, median_filter, dtw_cpu,
    merge_punctuations)
    in edytlab: crates/ml-whisper/src/{mel,decode,segments,timing}.rs

  Demucs - https://github.com/facebookresearch/demucs
    commit e976d93ecc3865e5757426930257e200846a520a
    MIT License, Copyright (c) Meta Platforms, Inc. and affiliates.
    derived: apply_model(split=True), center_trim, the normalisation of
    Separator.separate_tensor, HTDemucs _spec / _ispec / _magnitude / _mask,
    and the standardisation HTDemucs.forward does around the network
    in edytlab: crates/ml-demucs/src/{separate,spec,htdemucs}.rs

  pyannote.audio - https://github.com/pyannote/pyannote-audio
    version 3.3.2
    MIT License, Copyright (c) 2020 CNRS
    derived: Inference.aggregate and Inference.trim, SpeakerDiarizationMixin
    (speaker_count, to_diarization), AgglomerativeClustering, BaseClustering
    and the speaker-diarization-3.1 settings
    in edytlab: crates/ml-diarize/src/{cluster,frames,pipeline}.rs, and
    crates/ml-diarize/tests/fixtures/reference.py (copied, with only the
    torch types taken out; a test reference, not part of an installer)

  SciPy - https://github.com/scipy/scipy
    scipy.cluster.hierarchy.linkage (method="centroid") and fcluster, from
    _hierarchy.pyx: the merge order and the numbering of clusters
    BSD 3-Clause License, Copyright (c) 2001-2002 Enthought, Inc.
    2003-2024, SciPy Developers.
    in edytlab: crates/ml-diarize/src/cluster.rs

  torchaudio and Kaldi - https://github.com/pytorch/audio,
  https://github.com/kaldi-asr/kaldi
    torchaudio.compliance.kaldi.fbank, which is Kaldi's filterbank feature
    BSD 2-Clause License, Copyright (c) 2017 Facebook Inc. (Soumith
    Chintala) (torchaudio); Apache License 2.0, Copyright Kaldi contributors
    (Kaldi; see its legal notice below)
    in edytlab: crates/ml-diarize/src/fbank.rs. The settings are those of the
    WeSpeaker recipe (https://github.com/wenet-e2e/wespeaker, Apache-2.0).

B. Changes edytlab made to the code in section A
------------------------------------------------

edytlab changed all of it: it is translated from Python (PyTorch, NumPy, SciPy,
numba) into Rust, works on plain arrays and takes the model's outputs from
ONNX Runtime instead of PyTorch. Where an edytlab file does anything other
than what the upstream function does, its documentation says what and why.
Each upstream function is checked in edytlab's tests against numbers the
upstream code itself produced.

C. Models the app can download when the person asks
---------------------------------------------------

None of these is part of an installer. They are fetched only when someone
clicks Download in Settings, unchanged, from huggingface.co, and checked
against the size and SHA-256 recorded in crates/ml-pipeline/models.json.

  Whisper base (speech to text)
    onnx-community/whisper-base_timestamped, an ONNX export of OpenAI Whisper
    (https://github.com/openai/whisper). Licence recorded for the files:
    Apache-2.0 (https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0).

  pyannote segmentation 3.0 (who speaks when)
    onnx-community/pyannote-segmentation-3.0, an ONNX export of
    pyannote/segmentation-3.0 by Herve Bredin (CNRS), MIT License
    (https://opensource.org/license/MIT), Copyright (c) 2020 CNRS, trained
    on data that includes AMI, DIHARD and VoxConverse.

  WeSpeaker ResNet34-LM (speaker voiceprints)
    Wespeaker/wespeaker-voxceleb-resnet34-LM, by the WeSpeaker authors,
    licensed under Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International
    (https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/). edytlab does not modify
    the file; it is used as published.

D. Test data (in the source repository only, not in an installer)
-----------------------------------------------------------------

  AMI Meeting Corpus, 40 seconds of ES2004a, Copyright (c) the AMI Consortium,
  Creative Commons Attribution 4.0 International, cut to length by edytlab;
  see crates/ml-diarize/tests/fixtures/AMI-LICENSE.md.
  The word-timing evaluation (scripts/word-timing-eval) downloads LibriSpeech
  dev-clean (CC BY 4.0) when run; nothing of it is kept in the repository.
  Stem separation fixture, four stems of 8.5 seconds each, cut and levelled by
  edytlab (crates/ml-demucs/tests/fixtures/stems/): a solo singer from
  VocalSet (Wilkins, Seetharaman, Wahl and Pardo, ISMIR 2018,
  https://doi.org/10.5281/zenodo.1193957), Creative Commons Attribution 4.0
  International; and three loops from Freesound, dedicated to the public
  domain under CC0 1.0 by their authors blakengouda (573948), josefpres
  (483146) and josefpres (483627). See the README beside the files.

E. Software that ships beside edytlab with notices of its own
-------------------------------------------------------------

  ONNX Runtime, Copyright (c) Microsoft Corporation, MIT License. The
  installers carry it with its LICENSE and ThirdPartyNotices.txt in the
  licenses/onnxruntime/ folder next to this file.
  Microsoft Visual C++ runtime (Windows installers only): see
  licenses/vc-runtime/NOTICE.txt next to this file.

Apache License 2.0 - Kaldi (its COPYING file, legal notice first)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

 Update to legal notice, made Feb 2012, modified Sep 2013.  We would like to
 clarify that we are using a convention where multiple names in the Apache
 copyright headers, for example

  // Copyright 2009-2012  Yanmin Qian  Arnab Ghoshal
  //                2013  Vassil Panayotov

 does not signify joint ownership of copyright of that file, except in cases
 where all those names were present in the original release made in March 2011--
 you can use the version history to work this out, if this matters to you.
 Instead, we intend that those contributors who later modified the file, agree
 to release their changes under the Apache license.  The conventional way of
 signifying this is to duplicate the Apache headers at the top of each file each
 time a change is made by a different author, but this would quickly become
 impractical.

 Where the copyright header says something like:

 // Copyright    2013   Johns Hopkins University (author: Daniel Povey)

 it is because the individual who wrote the code was at that institution as an
 employee, so the copyright is owned by the university (and we will have checked
 that the contributions were in accordance with the open-source policies of the
 institutions concerned, including getting them vetted individually where
 necessary).  From a legal point of view the copyright ownership is that of the
 institution concerned, and the (author: xxx) in parentheses is just
 informational, to identify the actual person who wrote the code, and is not
 intended to have any legal implications.  In some cases, however, particularly
 early on, we just wrote the name of the university or company concerned,
 without the actual author's name in parentheses.  If you see something like

 //  Copyright  2009-2012   Arnab Ghoshal  Microsoft Corporation

 it does not imply that Arnab was working for Microsoft, it is because someone
 else contributed to the file while working at Microsoft (this would be Daniel
 Povey, in fact, who was working at Microsoft Research at the outset of the
 project).

 The list of authors of each file is in an essentially arbitrary order, but is
 often chronological if they contributed in different years.

 The original legal notice is below.  Note: we are continuing to modify it by
 adding the names of new contributors, but at any given time, the list may
 be out of date.

JAVASCRIPT PACKAGES AND FONTS
=============================

The window of the app is a web page built from the packages below. This is
what the production build contains (the JavaScript that ended up in the
bundle, and the fonts and style framework it pulls in). Packages used only
to build or test the app are not listed.

The Geist and Geist Mono fonts are under the SIL Open Font License 1.1.
They are shipped as the @fontsource-variable packages publish them, in
WOFF2 form, not changed by edytlab, and neither declares a Reserved Font
Name, so the licence asks nothing beyond keeping its text and copyright
line with them, which follows.

The goals of the Open Font License (OFL) are to stimulate worldwide
development of collaborative font projects, to support the font creation
efforts of academic and linguistic communities, and to provide a free and
open framework in which fonts may be shared and improved in partnership
with others.

The OFL allows the licensed fonts to be used, studied, modified and
redistributed freely as long as they are not sold by themselves. The
fonts, including any derivative works, can be bundled, embedded,
redistributed and/or sold with any software provided that any reserved
names are not used by derivative works. The fonts and derivatives,
however, cannot be released under any other type of license. The
requirement for fonts to remain under this license does not apply
to any document created using the fonts or their derivatives.

DEFINITIONS
"Font Software" refers to the set of files released by the Copyright
Holder(s) under this license and clearly marked as such. This may
include source files, build scripts and documentation.

"Reserved Font Name" refers to any names specified as such after the
copyright statement(s).

"Original Version" refers to the collection of Font Software components as
distributed by the Copyright Holder(s).

"Modified Version" refers to any derivative made by adding to, deleting,
or substituting -- in part or in whole -- any of the components of the
Original Version, by changing formats or by porting the Font Software to a
new environment.

"Author" refers to any designer, engineer, programmer, technical
writer or other person who contributed to the Font Software.

PERMISSION & CONDITIONS
Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any person obtaining
a copy of the Font Software, to use, study, copy, merge, embed, modify,
redistribute, and sell modified and unmodified copies of the Font
Software, subject to the following conditions:

1) Neither the Font Software nor any of its individual components,
in Original or Modified Versions, may be sold by itself.

2) Original or Modified Versions of the Font Software may be bundled,
redistributed and/or sold with any software, provided that each copy
contains the above copyright notice and this license. These can be
included either as stand-alone text files, human-readable headers or
in the appropriate machine-readable metadata fields within text or
binary files as long as those fields can be easily viewed by the user.

3) No Modified Version of the Font Software may use the Reserved Font
Name(s) unless explicit written permission is granted by the corresponding
Copyright Holder. This restriction only applies to the primary font name as
presented to the users.

4) The name(s) of the Copyright Holder(s) or the Author(s) of the Font
Software shall not be used to promote, endorse or advertise any
Modified Version, except to acknowledge the contribution(s) of the
Copyright Holder(s) and the Author(s) or with their explicit written
permission.

5) The Font Software, modified or unmodified, in part or in whole,
must be distributed entirely under this license, and must not be
distributed under any other license. The requirement for fonts to
remain under this license does not apply to any document created
using the Font Software.

not met.

RUST CRATES
===========

The edytlab desktop app (crate edytlab-desktop) is linked against the crates
below, for the Linux, Windows and macOS builds it ships (x86_64 and aarch64).
Development- and build-time-only crates are not listed: they are not part of
the program that is shipped.

Where a crate offers a choice of licences ("MIT OR Apache-2.0"), edytlab uses
it under the first of its licences in this order: MIT, Apache-2.0,
BSD-3-Clause, ISC, Zlib, Unicode-3.0, MPL-2.0, CDLA-Permissive-2.0. A line
"declared:" below shows the crate's own SPDX expression.

The text below each heading is the licence file the crate ships, one block per
distinct text, so that a copyright line a crate's file carries is kept. Where
a crate ships no licence file ("no licence file in the crate"), the standard
SPDX text of its licence is given and the crate's Cargo.toml says who holds
the copyright.

Licences in use
---------------
426 x MIT License (MIT)
19 x ISC License (ISC)
19 x Unicode License v3 (Unicode-3.0)
17 x Mozilla Public License 2.0 (MPL-2.0)
12 x Apache License 2.0 (Apache-2.0)
7 x BSD 3-Clause "New" or "Revised" License (BSD-3-Clause)
2 x zlib License (Zlib)
1 x Creative Commons Zero v1.0 Universal (CC0-1.0)
1 x Community Data License Agreement Permissive 2.0 (CDLA-Permissive-2.0)

Apache License 2.0 (Apache-2.0)

/// The PUA code points special-cased in the GB18030 encoder.
pub(crate) static GB18030_2022_OVERRIDE_PUA: [u16; 18] = [
    0xE78D, 0xE78E, 0xE78F, 0xE790, 0xE791, 0xE792, 0xE793, 0xE794, 0xE795, 0xE796, 0xE81E, 0xE826,
    0xE82B, 0xE82C, 0xE832, 0xE843, 0xE854, 0xE864,
];

/// The bytes corresponding to the PUA code points special-cased in the GB18030 encoder.
pub(crate) static GB18030_2022_OVERRIDE_BYTES: [[u8; 2]; 18] = [
    [0xA6, 0xD9],
    [0xA6, 0xDA],
    [0xA6, 0xDB],
    [0xA6, 0xDC],
    [0xA6, 0xDD],
    [0xA6, 0xDE],
    [0xA6, 0xDF],
    [0xA6, 0xEC],
    [0xA6, 0xED],
    [0xA6, 0xF3],
    [0xFE, 0x59],
    [0xFE, 0x61],
    [0xFE, 0x66],
    [0xFE, 0x67],
    [0xFE, 0x6D],
    [0xFE, 0x7E],
    [0xFE, 0x90],
    [0xFE, 0xA0],
];

#ifndef OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H
#define OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H

#include "../../limbs/limbs.h"

#define P384_LIMBS (384u / LIMB_BITS)

typedef struct {
  Limb X[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Y[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Z[P384_LIMBS];
} P384_POINT;

typedef struct {
  Limb X[P384_LIMBS];
  Limb Y[P384_LIMBS];
} P384_POINT_AFFINE;


#endif // OPENSSL_HEADER_EC_ECP_NISTZ384_H

//! EdDSA Signatures.

use super::ops::ELEM_LEN;
use crate::digest;

pub mod signing;
pub mod verification;

/// The length of an Ed25519 public key.
pub const ED25519_PUBLIC_KEY_LEN: usize = ELEM_LEN;

pub fn eddsa_digest(signature_r: &[u8], public_key: &[u8], msg: &[u8]) -> digest::Digest {
    let mut ctx = digest::Context::new(&digest::SHA512);
    ctx.update(signature_r);
    ctx.update(public_key);
    ctx.update(msg);
    ctx.finish()
}

use crate::limb::Limb;

#[derive(Clone, Copy)]
#[repr(transparent)]
pub struct N0([Limb; 2]);

impl N0 {
    #[cfg(feature = "alloc")]
    pub(super) const LIMBS_USED: usize = 64 / crate::limb::LIMB_BITS;

    #[inline]
    pub const fn precalculated(n0: u64) -> Self {
        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "64")]
        {
            Self([n0, 0])
        }

        #[cfg(target_pointer_width = "32")]
        {
            Self([n0 as Limb, (n0 >> crate::limb::LIMB_BITS) as Limb])
        }
    }
}

use crate::error::{KeyRejected, Unspecified};

impl From<untrusted::EndOfInput> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: untrusted::EndOfInput) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From<core::array::TryFromSliceError> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: core::array::TryFromSliceError) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

impl From<KeyRejected> for Unspecified {
    fn from(source: KeyRejected) -> Self {
        super::erase(source)
    }
}

#![allow(clippy::all)]
// Copied from regex_syntax crate to avoid pulling in the whole crate just for a utility function
// https://github.com/rust-lang/regex/blob/431c4e4867e1eb33eb39b23ed47c9934b2672f8f/regex-syntax/src/lib.rs
//
// Copyright (c) 2014 The Rust Project Developers
//
// Permission is hereby granted, free of charge, to any
// person obtaining a copy of this software and associated
// documentation files (the "Software"), to deal in the
// Software without restriction, including without
// limitation the rights to use, copy, modify, merge,
// publish, distribute, sublicense, and/or sell copies of
// the Software, and to permit persons to whom the Software
// is furnished to do so, subject to the following
// conditions:
//
// The above copyright notice and this permission notice
// shall be included in all copies or substantial portions
// of the Software.
//
// THE SOFTWARE IS PROVIDED "AS IS", WITHOUT WARRANTY OF
// ANY KIND, EXPRESS OR IMPLIED, INCLUDING BUT NOT LIMITED
// TO THE WARRANTIES OF MERCHANTABILITY, FITNESS FOR A
// PARTICULAR PURPOSE AND NONINFRINGEMENT. IN NO EVENT
// SHALL THE AUTHORS OR COPYRIGHT HOLDERS BE LIABLE FOR ANY
// CLAIM, DAMAGES OR OTHER LIABILITY, WHETHER IN AN ACTION
// OF CONTRACT, TORT OR OTHERWISE, ARISING FROM, OUT OF OR
// IN CONNECTION WITH THE SOFTWARE OR THE USE OR OTHER
// DEALINGS IN THE SOFTWARE.

pub fn escape(text: &str) -> String {
    let mut quoted = String::new();
    escape_into(text, &mut quoted);
    quoted
}

fn escape_into(text: &str, buf: &mut String) {
    buf.reserve(text.len());
    for c in text.chars() {
        if is_meta_character(c) {
            buf.push('\\');
        }
        buf.push(c);
    }
}

fn is_meta_character(c: char) -> bool {
    match c {
        '\\' | '.' | '+' | '*' | '?' | '(' | ')' | '|' | '[' | ']' | '{' | '}' | '^' | '$'
        | '#' | '&' | '-' | '~' => true,
        _ => false,
    }
}
